# Part E — Business Intelligence and Business Graph APIs (UNMOUNTED)

## SCAN E: Business Intelligence (BI) and Business Graph (BG) HTTP APIs

Repo: <repo>, checkout = origin/main 5f176a2. Read-only scan; nothing executed. All paths below are relative to the repo root unless prefixed. "Inferred" = derived from reading code/Python/Django/DRF semantics, never run.

DRF/Django source used for framework-behaviour claims: DRF 3.15.2 and Django 4.2.27 in `<venv>/lib/python3.12/site-packages/` (same pins as requirements.txt).

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
### (1) SCOPE

Two self-contained DRF modules: `app/business_intelligence/{urls,api}.py` (11 method-endpoints on 7 route patterns) and `app/business_graph/{urls,api}.py` (11 method-endpoints on 10 route patterns). **MOUNT PREFIX: NONE EXISTS.** Neither urls.py is included by `app/urls.py` or `bizora/urls.py` (ground truth #1), so every endpoint here is **UNMOUNTED**: no HTTP request can reach it today. All paths in this file are RELATIVE to the respective urls.py. In addition, even if mounted, every call would return HTTP 403 as written, because the tenant is read only from `request.business` / `request.auth_business`, and nothing in the repo sets either attribute (grep over *.py: only `app/business_intelligence/api.py:77`, `app/business_graph/api.py:158` read it; only tests assign `req.business = biz`, e.g. `app/business_graph/tests/test_api_and_scenarios.py:112-206`). The routes carry NO `business_id` in the path (unlike the LIVE domain views). The BI analysis/calculation modules (attribution, revenue, delivery, support, website/conversion, campaign, customer/product/retention, anomalies) and the graph incremental-sync/projection helpers have NO HTTP surface (section 4). No celery/RQ/scheduler exists (requirements.txt: Django, DRF, psycopg, pytz, networkx only); the container runs `manage.py runserver` (Dockerfile CMD), so every endpoint below is synchronous in-request with no timeout/queue.

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
### (2a) SHARED AUTH / ERROR BEHAVIOUR (applies to every endpoint in both modules)

**Permission class.** Both base views set `permission_classes = [HasAuthorizedBusiness]` (BI `app/business_intelligence/api.py:120-123`; BG `app/business_graph/api.py:201-204`). That REPLACES the project default `IsAuthenticated` (`bizora/settings.py:177-179`). So these views never require a logged-in user; they only require a resolvable `request.business`. No membership/role/BusinessAccess check exists (the LIVE domain views use those; these do not). No `DEFAULT_AUTHENTICATION_CLASSES` is configured (`bizora/settings.py:168-180`), so DRF defaults to SessionAuthentication then BasicAuthentication (DRF `settings.py:40-43`). CSRF is enforced by SessionAuthentication only when a session user is authenticated on POST/PATCH; unauthenticated calls skip it (DRF behaviour, inferred).

**`resolve_authorized_business(request)`** (BI `api.py:70-106`; BG `api.py:151-187`; the two copies are identical):
| Step | Condition | Exception message (PermissionDenied) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `getattr(request,"business",None) or getattr(request,"auth_business",None)` is None/falsy | "Access denied: No authorized business scope." |
| 2 | value is not a `models.Business` and is an `int` but no Business with that pk | "Access denied: Authorized business not found." |
| 3 | value is neither `Business` nor `int` | "Access denied: Invalid business context." |
| 4 | `business_id` found in `request.query_params` (first) or, if absent there and `request.data` is a dict, in `request.data`; `int(value) != biz.pk` | "Access denied: Caller-supplied business_id does not match authorized business." |
| 5 | same `business_id` present but `int()` raises ValueError/TypeError (e.g. `?business_id=` empty string) | "Access denied: Malformed business_id parameter." |
Accepted `request.business` types: a `Business` instance or an integer pk, set as an attribute on the Django HttpRequest (DRF `Request.__getattr__` proxies to it, DRF `request.py:414-423`).

**What the HTTP client actually sees (inferred from code + DRF source):** `HasAuthorizedBusiness.has_permission` (BI `api.py:109-117`, BG `api.py:190-198`) catches `PermissionDenied` and returns False, so the five messages above NEVER reach an HTTP response; DRF's `permission_denied` (DRF `views.py:169-175`) raises instead:
- no authenticated user: `NotAuthenticated`; the first authenticator (SessionAuthentication) defines no `authenticate_header`, so status is coerced to **403** (DRF `views.py:453-461`); body `{"detail": "Authentication credentials were not provided."}`.
- authenticated session/basic user but no business: **403** `{"detail": "You do not have permission to perform this action."}`.
- Malformed JSON body: `request.data` is touched inside the permission check (`api.py:94`), so a `ParseError` surfaces as standard DRF **400** `{"detail": "JSON parse error - ..."}`.

**Error shapes (two different families):**
- BI (`BaseIntelligenceAPIView.handle_exception`, `app/business_intelligence/api.py:148-162`), deliberately GENERIC (SEC-MED-1):
  - `FactScopeError | IssueScopeError | AnomalyScopeError` -> **403** `{"error": "The requested resource was not found or access is denied."}`
  - `FactValidationError | IssueValidationError | AnomalyValidationError | MetricValidationError | RelationshipValidationError` -> **400** `{"error": "Invalid request parameters or unresolvable resource reference."}`
  - direct 404s in views use `{"error": "The requested resource was not found or access is denied."}` (api.py:224-227, 340-343, 463-466).
  - NOT handled (propagate -> HTTP 500, not JSON): `MetricSnapshotConflictError`, `RelationshipConflictError` (both plain `ValueError` subclasses, not subclasses of the handled ones), `decimal.InvalidOperation`, `AttributeError`/`TypeError` from bad body types, `IntegrityError`.
- BG (`BaseGraphAPIView.handle_exception`, `app/business_graph/api.py:209-214`), SPECIFIC (`str(exc)` returned):
  - `GraphScopeError` -> **403** `{"error": "<message>"}`; checked first because it subclasses `GraphValidationError` (`app/business_graph/projection.py:40-49`).
  - `GraphValidationError | GraphCrossBusinessError` -> **400** `{"error": "<message>"}`.
  - Cross-tenant messages embed the OWNING business id, e.g. "Customer #5 belongs to business 7, not 3." (`journeys.py:202`, `projection.py:211-213`, `snapshots.py:267-269`, `traversal.py:289-291`). This leaks tenant ids (contrast with BI).
  - BG never returns 404; "not found" is 400 (e.g. "GraphSnapshot #9 does not exist.", `snapshots.py:264`).
  - Unhandled -> 500: `ValueError` from bare `int()` on query params (`api.py:236-237, 302, 343, 345, 525`), `ValueError` from `parse_datetime` on well-formed-but-invalid datetimes (Django `dateparse.py:104-129`), `TypeError` comparing naive vs aware datetimes (inferred: Python semantics, see 2d), `AssertionError` for negative `limit` in query relationship mode (inferred).

**Renderers/parsers** (`bizora/settings.py:168-176`): JSON only (+Browsable API when DEBUG); request parsers JSON, Form, MultiPart. Several fields below are only correct when sent as JSON (booleans, lists, objects). No throttling configured.

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
### (2b) ENDPOINT TABLE: BUSINESS INTELLIGENCE (relative to `app/business_intelligence/urls.py:7-15`)

Auth for all rows: `HasAuthorizedBusiness` (see 2a). Common query param on all: optional `business_id` guard (must equal authorized business, else 403). "Generic 400/403/404" = the BI generic bodies in 2a. Pagination envelope (rows marked P): `{"total_count","limit","offset","results":[...]}`; `limit` default 50, clamped to 1..200; `offset` default 0, min 0; if either is non-integer BOTH reset to 50/0 silently (`api.py:128-146`).

| ID | Method | Path (relative) | Status | Query params | Request body | Response (success) | Errors | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BI-1 | GET | `metrics/` (name `intelligence-metrics`) | UNMOUNTED | `limit`,`offset` (P). No category/search filter. Ordered by `key` asc | none | 200 P envelope; each result `{id, metric_key, name, category, unit, value_type}` | generic 400 if business missing; 500 on `MetricSnapshotConflictError` | urls.py:8; api.py:191-214 |
| BI-2 | GET | `metrics/<str:metric_key>/snapshots/` (`intelligence-metric-snapshots`) | UNMOUNTED | `period_type`, `dimension_type` (exact match, NOT validated; unknown value -> empty list), `limit`,`offset` (P). Ordered `-period_start` (no tiebreak) | none | 200 `{metric_key, total_count, limit, offset, results:[{id, value, period_type, period_start, period_end, dimension_type, dimension_value}]}` | 404 `{"error": generic}` if no BusinessMetric row for (business,key) | urls.py:9; api.py:217-260 |
| BI-3 | GET | `facts/` (`intelligence-facts`) | UNMOUNTED | `status` (upper-cased), `fact_type` (exact), `limit`,`offset` (P). Ordered `-detected_at` (no tiebreak) | none | 200 P envelope; result `{id, fact_type, statement, value, value_unit, confidence, status, rule_key, detected_at, evidence}` | - | urls.py:10; api.py:266-298 |
| BI-4 | POST | `facts/` | UNMOUNTED | - | `statement` (req), `fact_type`, `value`, `value_unit`, `rule_key`, `evidence` | **201** `{id, fact_type, statement, status, created:true}` or **200** same with `created:false` (upsert) | generic 400 blank/missing statement; 500 non-numeric `value` | api.py:300-329 |
| BI-5 | GET | `facts/<int:fact_id>/` (`intelligence-fact-detail`) | UNMOUNTED | - | none | 200 `{id, business_id, fact_type, statement, value, value_unit, confidence, status, rule_key, valid_from, valid_to, detected_at, evidence}` | 404 generic (missing, other tenant, OR fact not VERIFIED/in-window) | urls.py:11; api.py:335-359 |
| BI-6 | PATCH | `facts/<int:fact_id>/` | UNMOUNTED | - | `status` (req), `reason` | 200 `{id, status, valid_to, updated_at}` | 400 generic (bad status OR id unknown OR fact hidden by manager); 403 generic cross-tenant | api.py:361-377 |
| BI-7 | GET | `issues/` (`intelligence-issues`) | UNMOUNTED | `status`,`severity` (both upper-cased), `issue_type` (exact), `limit`,`offset` (P). Ordered `-detected_at` | none | 200 P envelope; result `{id, issue_type, title, description, severity, confidence, status, detected_at, resolved_at, metadata}` | - | urls.py:12; api.py:383-419 |
| BI-8 | POST | `issues/` | UNMOUNTED | - | `issue_type`,`title` (req), `description`,`severity`,`confidence`,`rule_key`,`fact_ids`,`snapshot_ids` | **201** `{id, issue_type, title, status, created:true}` or **200** `created:false` (upsert) | generic 400 blank title / bad severity / confidence outside 0..1; 500 non-numeric confidence, missing `issue_type` (inferred NOT NULL) | api.py:421-453 |
| BI-9 | GET | `issues/<int:issue_id>/` (`intelligence-issue-detail`) | UNMOUNTED | - | none | 200 `{id, business_id, issue_type, title, description, severity, confidence, status, rule_key, detected_at, resolved_at, metadata}` | 404 generic | urls.py:13; api.py:459-481 |
| BI-10 | PATCH | `issues/<int:issue_id>/` | UNMOUNTED | - | `status` (req), `notes` | 200 `{id, status, resolved_at, updated_at}` | 400 generic (bad status OR unknown id); 403 generic cross-tenant | api.py:483-499 |
| BI-11 | POST | `pipeline/run/` (`intelligence-pipeline-run`) | UNMOUNTED | - | none read (body ignored except optional `business_id` guard) | 200 `{business_id, relationships_reconciled:{created,updated,unchanged,invalidated}, quarantined_records, active_facts_count, open_issues_count, status:"COMPLETED"}` | 500 on `RelationshipConflictError` / `MetricSnapshotConflictError`; see 2c | urls.py:14; api.py:502-529 |

Methods not defined on any BI route (DRF returns 405 `{"detail":"Method \"X\" not allowed."}`): PUT, DELETE everywhere; POST on `metrics/` and detail routes; PATCH on list routes.

#### BI field-level detail

**Number/date formats.** Decimals are serialised as strings via `str()` (e.g. test asserts `"1500.000000"`, `app/business_intelligence/tests/test_api_and_pipeline.py:124`; `confidence` as `"1.0000"`-style). Datetimes are `.isoformat()` of timezone-aware UTC values (`USE_TZ=True`, `bizora/settings.py:155`), e.g. `2026-01-05T00:00:00+00:00`. `value`/`valid_*`/`resolved_at` null -> JSON null.

**BI-1 behaviour:** `GET` WRITES. It calls `sync_metric_registry(business_id)` (api.py:196) which creates/repairs one `BusinessMetric` row per registry definition (42 rows, section 3) and deactivates retired service-owned rows (`metrics.py:1154-1226`). The listing then returns EVERY `BusinessMetric` row of the business, without filtering `is_active` or key prefix (api.py:197), so legacy rows such as key `DAILY_PERFORMANCE` created by `app/services/bi.py:66-70` would appear too. Response omits `description`, `is_active`. After the first call 42 registry rows exist (default `limit=50` fits them all when no legacy rows exist).

**BI-2 behaviour:** The metric must already exist as a DB row (lookup `BusinessMetric(business, key)`, api.py:222), so a freshly created business gets 404 for every key until BI-1 or BI-11 has run `sync_metric_registry`. The path param is not validated against the registry; a retired/inactive key that still has a row works. NO default scoping: without `dimension_type` the list mixes the business-wide series with every dimensional series (the service-level `list_metric_snapshots` defaults to business-wide only, `metrics.py:1410-1495`, but the view does not use it). No `dimension_value`, date-window, `calculation_rule` filters. NOT returned: `calculation_rule`, `calculated_at`, `metadata` (revision/supersedes/inputs/data_available), metric `unit`/`value_type` (take those from BI-1). No endpoint can create a snapshot (BI-2 is read-only), and no HTTP path calls any calculator, so this list is empty unless a script/service writes rows (section 4).

**BI-3/BI-5 visibility rule (important):** the views query `models.BusinessFact.objects`, whose default manager is `BusinessFactManager` -> `get_queryset().active_for_context()` (`app/models.py:1067-1087`): only rows with `status='VERIFIED'` AND (`valid_from` null or <= now) AND (`valid_to` null or >= now). Consequences (inferred, not executed): `?status=CANDIDATE|INVALIDATED|EXPIRED` always returns an empty `results`; GET of an INVALIDATED/EXPIRED/CANDIDATE fact returns 404; `fact_type` filter works only among visible facts. `evidence` = the `metadata` JSON column (`f.metadata or {}`), which holds whatever BI-4 received in `evidence` plus a `status_change_reason` key if BI-6 was given a `reason`.

**BI-4 request fields (read from `api.py:300-329`, validated in `facts_issues.py:123-203`):**
| Field | Type | Required | Validation / default |
|---|---|---|---|
| `statement` | string | yes | must be non-empty after `.strip()` else generic 400; non-string -> `AttributeError` -> 500 |
| `fact_type` | string | no | default `"GENERAL"` (api.py:303); NOT validated against documented types; DB max 100 |
| `value` | number or numeric string | no | `Decimal(str(value))` (api.py:305); non-numeric -> `InvalidOperation` -> 500; stored `Decimal(24,6)` nullable |
| `value_unit` | string | no | DB max 30; not validated |
| `rule_key` | string | no | DB max 100; part of the dedup key |
| `evidence` | JSON object | no | default `{}`; stored verbatim as `metadata`; must be object (non-object -> 500) |
| `business_id` | int | no | tenant guard only |
Not accepted from the client (forced by the view): `status` always `VERIFIED`, `confidence` always `1.0000`, `valid_from/valid_to` null, `subject_entity_type/id` null, `detected_at` = now. **Upsert key** (`facts_issues.py:161-170`): (business, `fact_type`, `rule_key`, subject null, `valid_from` null, `valid_to` null) among visible facts. Django treats `rule_key=None` as `IS NULL`, so two POSTs with the same `fact_type` and NO `rule_key` overwrite one another (statement, value, unit, evidence replaced; `detected_at` reset; response 200, `created:false`). A UI that wants distinct facts must send a unique `rule_key`. Because the view cannot set `subject_entity_*`, no API-created fact can ever attach to a graph node (affects BG-2 `explain`).

**BI-6 `status`:** one of `CANDIDATE`, `VERIFIED`, `INVALIDATED`, `EXPIRED` (case-insensitive; `facts_issues.py:217-219`); missing/blank -> generic 400. `EXPIRED` also sets `valid_to=now` when it was null (`:236-237`). `reason` (optional) is saved in `metadata["status_change_reason"]`. No transition rules (any->any). Because the lookup uses the same visible-only manager (`:221`), a fact that is not currently VERIFIED/in-window yields "does not exist" (generic 400): after a PATCH to anything other than `VERIFIED`, the fact vanishes from BI-3/BI-5 and can never be PATCHed back through the API. Unknown id -> 400 but cross-tenant id -> 403: these two are distinguishable, so the SEC-MED-1 "anti-oracle" claim (api.py:12) is only true for GET (both 404).

**BI-7 notes:** unlike facts, issues use the plain default manager, so all four statuses are listable. List items omit `rule_key`; detail (BI-9) includes it. `metadata` carries `fact_ids` (sorted unique ints), `snapshot_ids`, `resolution_notes` when supplied; it is `{}` when null.

**BI-8 request fields (`api.py:421-453`, `facts_issues.py:243-342`):**
| Field | Type | Required | Validation / default |
|---|---|---|---|
| `issue_type` | string | yes (in practice) | NOT validated by the service; DB `NOT NULL` max 100; omission -> IntegrityError -> 500 (inferred). Documented values in section 3, not enforced |
| `title` | string | yes | non-blank else generic 400; DB max 255 (no length check) |
| `description` | string | no | on update, kept if omitted (`description or existing`) |
| `severity` | string | no | default `"MEDIUM"`; upper-cased; must be in `ISSUE_SEVERITIES` else generic 400; non-string -> 500 |
| `confidence` | number or numeric string | no | default `"1.0000"`; `Decimal(str(x))`; must be 0..1 inclusive else generic 400; non-numeric -> 500 |
| `rule_key` | string | no | part of dedup key |
| `fact_ids`, `snapshot_ids` | list of ints | no | stored as sorted unique lists in `metadata`; NOT checked for existence or tenant ownership; non-list/mixed types may 500 |
Not accepted: `status` (always `OPEN` on create), `subject_entity_*` (null), `detected_at` (now), `metadata`. **Upsert:** an existing OPEN or ACKNOWLEDGED issue with identical (business, `issue_type`, `rule_key`, subject null/null) is updated in place (title, description, severity, confidence, `detected_at`; id lists merged) -> 200, `created:false`. Otherwise a new OPEN row -> 201. Extra model-level quirk (`app/models.py:1154-1173`): `BusinessIssue.save()` makes any new OPEN issue adopt the pk of an existing OPEN issue with the same (business, `issue_type`, subject) EVEN IF `rule_key` differs, yet the API still reports `created:true`/201.

**BI-10 `status`:** `OPEN`, `ACKNOWLEDGED`, `RESOLVED`, `DISMISSED` (case-insensitive). `resolved_at` = now for RESOLVED/DISMISSED, cleared (null) for OPEN/ACKNOWLEDGED. `notes` -> `metadata["resolution_notes"]`. Any transition allowed including reopen.

#### 2c. BI-11 pipeline run: what it executes
Code: `PipelineOrchestrationView.post`, `app/business_intelligence/api.py:502-529`. **Synchronous**, single HTTP request, no queue/thread/celery, no timeout, 200 on success (never 202). Order:
1. `reconcile_relationships_with_quarantine(business_id)` (api.py:165-188) -> `rebuild_relationships` (`relationships.py:309-364`): ONE `transaction.atomic()` that row-locks the Business (`select_for_update`), loads every Customer, Product, Order, Payment, Shipment, Conversation, Call, Campaign, WebsiteVisitor, WebsiteSession of the business plus OrderItem, ConversationParticipant, Message, WebsiteEvent, CampaignConversion children into memory, validates every referenced row (per-row `filter(pk=...).first()` for uncached rows), derives candidate links from the 23 fixed rules (`relationships.py:74-98`), then creates/updates `BusinessRelationship` rows and sets `valid_to=now` on service-owned links no longer supported (never deletes). Writes: `business_relationships`. Run time grows linearly with (and is unbounded in) business data volume; actual duration NOT VERIFIED (not executed).
   - `RelationshipValidationError` (bad/missing/cross-business reference) is CAUGHT: the whole transaction rolled back (nothing written), logged server-side, and the API still returns 200 with all four counts 0, `quarantined_records: 1`, `status:"COMPLETED"`. The error text is NOT returned. Discrepancy: docstring says per-row quarantine (api.py:13-15); code quarantines the whole tenant rebuild.
   - `RelationshipConflictError` (tuple owned by another rule) is NOT caught -> HTTP 500 (non-JSON).
2. `sync_metric_registry(business_id)` (`metrics.py:1154-1226`): idempotent upsert of the 42 `BusinessMetric` rows; result ignored by the response. Writes: `business_metrics`.
3. Two counts: `active_facts_count` = `BusinessFact.objects.filter(business).count()` (visible/VERIFIED+in-window facts only, api.py:514); `open_issues_count` = issues with `status="OPEN"` only, excluding ACKNOWLEDGED (api.py:515).
It does NOT calculate any metric snapshot, run anomaly rules, or create/alter facts or issues, although the module docstring (api.py:19) and `docs/implementations/features/business-intelligence.md` "Intelligence Processing Flow" describe "canonical data -> relationships -> metrics -> anomalies -> facts & issues". `"status":"COMPLETED"` is a constant. Idempotent in effect (replays report `unchanged`). Concurrent runs for one business serialise on the Business row lock on PostgreSQL only; SQLite has no row locking (`metrics.py:106-108`).

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
### (2d) ENDPOINT TABLE: BUSINESS GRAPH (relative to `app/business_graph/urls.py:7-18`)

Auth for all rows: `HasAuthorizedBusiness` (see 2a); errors are the BG family (specific `{"error": "<message>"}` 400/403, 2a). Common optional `business_id` guard on all. Filter params (call them "FILTERS") are the ones parsed by `_parse_graph_filter` (`api.py:54-148`), listed once below the table.

| ID | Method | Path (relative) | Status | Query params | Request body | Response (success) | Errors | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BG-1 | GET | `traversal/` (`graph-traversal`) | UNMOUNTED | `root_entity_type` (req), `root_entity_id` (req int), `direction` (default `OUTBOUND`), `max_depth` (default 2), `max_nodes` (default 100), FILTERS | none | 200 `{business_id, root_node_id, depth_reached, truncated, node_count, edge_count, nodes[], edges[]}` (node/edge shapes below) | 400 missing params; 400 `root_entity_id must be an integer.`; 400/403 service messages; 500 non-int `max_depth`/`max_nodes` | urls.py:8; api.py:217-277; traversal.py:243-343 |
| BG-2 | GET | `paths/` (`graph-paths`) | UNMOUNTED | `start_entity_type`,`start_entity_id`,`end_entity_type`,`end_entity_id` (all req), `direction` (default `OUTBOUND`), `max_depth` (default 5), `explain` (default false), FILTERS | none | 200 `{business_id, paths_found, paths:[{length, nodes:[{id,entity_type,entity_id}], edges:[{id,relationship_type}], explanation?}]}` | 400 missing params; 400 `start_entity_id and end_entity_id must be integers.`; 400 start/end node not found; 500 non-int `max_depth` | urls.py:9; api.py:280-333; traversal.py:346-441 |
| BG-3 | GET | `query/` (`graph-query`) | UNMOUNTED | `entity_type`,`entity_id`,`relationship_type`,`limit` (default 100), FILTERS | none | 200 same envelope as BG-1 | 500 non-int `entity_id`/`limit`; see mode notes | urls.py:10; api.py:336-384; traversal.py:444-500 |
| BG-4 | GET | `journeys/customer/<int:customer_id>/` (`graph-customer-journey`) | UNMOUNTED | FILTERS | none | 200 `{business_id, customer_id, canonical_identity_id, node_count, edge_count, summary, timeline[]}` | 400 not found / not projected; 403 other tenant | urls.py:11; api.py:387-417; journeys.py:182-256 |
| BG-5 | GET | `journeys/order/<int:order_id>/` (`graph-order-journey`) | UNMOUNTED | FILTERS | none | 200 `{business_id, order_id, status, total_amount, customer_node_id, product_count, payment_count, shipment_count, timeline[]}` | same | urls.py:12; api.py:420-451; journeys.py:259-351 |
| BG-6 | GET | `journeys/product/<int:product_id>/` (`graph-product-journey`) | UNMOUNTED | FILTERS | none | 200 `{business_id, product_id, summary, timeline_length}` ONLY | same | urls.py:13; api.py:454-471; journeys.py:354-455 |
| BG-7 | GET | `journeys/campaign/<int:campaign_id>/` (`graph-campaign-journey`) | UNMOUNTED | FILTERS | none | 200 `{business_id, campaign_id, summary, timeline_length}` ONLY | same | urls.py:14; api.py:474-491; journeys.py:458-563 |
| BG-8 | GET | `snapshots/` (`graph-snapshots`) | UNMOUNTED | none (NO pagination, NO filters) | none | 200 bare JSON ARRAY `[{id, name, snapshot_type, node_count, edge_count, generated_at}]`, ordered `-generated_at` | - | urls.py:15; api.py:497-510 |
| BG-9 | POST | `snapshots/` | UNMOUNTED | - | `snapshot_type`,`name`,`root_entity_type`,`root_entity_id`,`filters` | **201** `{id, name, snapshot_type, node_count, edge_count, generated_at}` | 400 bad type / missing `root_entity_id` / journey errors; 403 other tenant; 500 non-int `root_entity_id` | api.py:512-539; snapshots.py:113-250 |
| BG-10 | GET | `snapshots/<int:snapshot_id>/` (`graph-snapshot-detail`) | UNMOUNTED | none | none | 200 `{id, business_id, name, snapshot_type, node_count, edge_count, filters, snapshot_data, generated_at}` | 400 `GraphSnapshot #N does not exist.` (not 404); 403 other tenant | urls.py:16; api.py:554-574; snapshots.py:253-271 |
| BG-11 | POST | `rebuild/` (`graph-rebuild`) | UNMOUNTED | - | `clean` | 200 `{business_id, nodes_created, nodes_updated, nodes_pruned, edges_created, edges_updated, edges_pruned, total_nodes, total_edges}` | 400/403 from projection errors (rolled back); see 2e | urls.py:17; api.py:577-598; sync.py:300-344 |

Methods not defined: PUT/PATCH/DELETE on all routes (405); there is no endpoint to delete or compare snapshots, to list/get a node or edge by id, or to read facts/issues/metrics from the graph.

#### BG shared response shapes
- **node** (traversal/query): `{id, entity_type, entity_id, label, properties}`. `id` is the GraphNode pk; `entity_id` is the canonical entity pk. `node_type` (always equal to `entity_type`), `canonical_identity_id`, `first_seen_at`, `last_seen_at`, `created_at` are NOT returned here (only inside `snapshot_data`).
- **edge** (traversal/query): `{id, source_node_id, target_node_id, relationship_type, confidence}` (`confidence` string like `"1.0000"`). `properties` (rule_key, relationship_source, metadata), `valid_from`, `valid_to`, `business_relationship_id` NOT returned (only inside `snapshot_data` and path `explanation`).
- **node.properties** are only the subset of `status, channel, priority, currency, provider, event_type, direction, total_amount` that exist as attributes on the source model (Decimals stringified), `projection.py:161-170`. **label** rules: `projection.py:128-158` (e.g. customer `display_name` or `Customer #pk`, order `order_number`, `Payment <amount> <currency>`, `Shipment (<provider>) #pk`, `<channel> Conversation #pk`).
- **Timeline event** (customer/order journeys): `{entity_type, entity_id, label, stage, timestamp (ISO or null), properties}` (customer includes `properties`; order omits it). `connecting_relationships` exists on the dataclass but is returned only inside snapshot timelines. Sorted ascending by (no-timestamp last, timestamp, stage, entity_type, entity_id) (`journeys.py:171-178`); timestamp = node `first_seen_at` or `created_at`.

#### FILTERS (query params for BG-1..BG-7; request-body keys for BG-9, from `api.py:54-148`)
| Param | Parsing | Applied to |
|---|---|---|
| `entity_types` | comma-separated string (GET) or JSON list (POST); upper-cased | neighbour nodes only (root never filtered): node.entity_type must be in set |
| `relationship_types` | same format; upper-cased | edges (DB-level `relationship_type__in`) |
| `min_confidence` | `Decimal(str(x))`; invalid value silently IGNORED | edges, `confidence >= x` |
| `status`,`channel`,`provider` | exact, case-sensitive string | neighbour `node.properties[key] == value` |
| `time_from`,`time_to` | `parse_datetime`; unparsable string silently ignored | `created_at` of neighbour nodes AND edges (row creation time, not event time) |
| `valid_at` | `parse_datetime` | edges: excluded if `valid_from > valid_at` or `valid_to < valid_at` |
| `dimensions` | only honoured when it is a JSON object (POST body); via GET it is a string and silently dropped | neighbour `node.properties[k] == v` for each pair (only the 8 property keys above exist, so it cannot filter by campaign/region/courier) |
Notes: `GraphFilter.node_properties/edge_properties` exist in the dataclass but no API param sets them (`traversal.py:55-56`). Datetimes must carry a timezone (`Z` or `%2B00:00`): a naive value (e.g. `2026-01-01` or `2026-01-01T00:00:00`) is compared with aware `created_at`/`valid_*` and raises `TypeError` -> 500 (inferred); an unencoded `+` in a URL becomes a space and the value is silently ignored. If ALL params are empty/invalid the filter is None (no filtering). Repeated GET params (`?entity_types=A&entity_types=B`) keep only the last value; use comma form.

#### BG-1 traversal details (`traversal.py:243-343`)
- `direction`: `OUTBOUND` | `INBOUND` | `BIDIRECTIONAL` (case-insensitive; `VALID_DIRECTIONS` `traversal.py:44`). Invalid -> 400 `Invalid direction 'X'; expected one of ['BIDIRECTIONAL', 'INBOUND', 'OUTBOUND']`.
- `max_depth` >= 0 (else 400 `max_depth must be non-negative`), `max_nodes` > 0 (else 400 `max_nodes must be positive`). NO upper cap on either in the API or service despite the docstring "strictly capped" (traversal.py:11-12). Traversal is BFS, one DB query per expanded node; `max_depth=0` returns only the root.
- `root_entity_type` is upper-cased and NOT validated against supported types; unknown root -> 400 `Root entity X #N not found in business B graph.`; root owned by another business -> 403 with owner id in the message.
- `truncated=true` when `max_nodes` was hit. Edges are appended BEFORE the node cap is checked, so when `truncated` is true some edges can reference node ids not present in `nodes`. When not truncated, edges only connect nodes in `nodes`. Edges between two nodes both sitting at the deepest level are not included (those nodes are not expanded).
- `depth_reached` = max depth actually dequeued. Ordering is deterministic: neighbours sorted by (`relationship_type`, edge `id`).

#### BG-2 path search details (`traversal.py:346-441`)
BFS shortest-first; returns at most **10** paths (service default `limit=10`, not exposed); cycles inside a path are skipped; `max_depth` must be > 0 else 400 `max_depth must be positive`. Start==end returns one path of `length` 0 with one node and no edges. No connection -> 200 with `paths_found: 0, paths: []`. `explain` is true for `"true"` or `"1"` (case-insensitive). With `explain`, each path gets `explanation` = list of steps `{node:{id, entity_type, entity_id, label, facts:[{fact_id, fact_type, statement, value, value_unit, confidence, status, rule_key}], issues:[{issue_id, issue_type, title, severity, status, confidence, description}]}, edge: null | {id, relationship_type, confidence, evidence: {} | {relationship_id, rule_key, relationship_source, confidence, valid_from, valid_to, metadata}}}` (`snapshots.py:274-402`). Facts shown are only VERIFIED in-window ones (same manager as BI-3); facts/issues attach by `subject_entity_type/id` which BI-4/BI-8 can never set, so via HTTP-created records `facts`/`issues` are always empty arrays. `evidence` is `{}` for edges with no `business_relationship`. One query per node (N+1).

#### BG-3 query modes (`traversal.py:444-500`)
- **Mode A** (`entity_type` AND `entity_id` both truthy): delegates to BG-1 with `direction=BIDIRECTIONAL`, `max_depth=2`, `max_nodes=limit`; `relationship_type` param is IGNORED in this mode. `limit <= 0` -> 400 `max_nodes must be positive`.
- **Mode B** (otherwise): edge-centric listing. `entity_type` alone is IGNORED; `relationship_type` exact match (NOT upper-cased); takes the first `limit` edges ordered by (`relationship_type`,`id`) BEFORE FILTERS are applied (so fewer than `limit` can come back); `root_node_id: 0`; `depth_reached` 1 or 0; `truncated` = `len(matched_edges) >= limit` (post-filter). `limit` negative/0 -> Django slice error / empty (inferred 500 for negative). No default tenant-wide paging or cursor: it is a single capped page, no offset.

#### Journeys (fixed server-side traversal, all `BIDIRECTIONAL`, FILTERS honoured)
| Journey | max_depth | max_nodes | summary keys |
|---|---|---|---|
| customer (`journeys.py:218-226`) | 3 | 500 | `total_nodes,total_edges,order_count,session_count,conversation_count,call_count,shipment_count,payment_count` |
| order (`:296-304`) | 2 | 200 | (none in API; API returns counts `product_count,payment_count,shipment_count` and `customer_node_id` = GraphNode pk) |
| product (`:391-399`) | 3 | 500 | `order_count,customer_count,campaign_count,website_count,shipment_count,payment_count,communication_count` |
| campaign (`:494-502`) | 4 | 500 | `session_count,conversion_count,customer_count,order_count,product_count,payment_count,shipment_count,communication_count` |
Journey prerequisites: the root entity must exist, belong to the business, AND already have a GraphNode (else 400 `Customer #N has not been projected into business B graph.`; same wording for Order/Product/Campaign) -> run BG-11 first. The API does not return a `truncated` flag. BG-6/BG-7 deliberately drop the timeline, nodes and edges (only `summary` + `timeline_length`); BG-4 returns the timeline but not nodes/edges; BG-5 returns `timeline` without per-event `properties`. `status` (BG-5) = node `properties.status` falling back to `order.status` or `"UNKNOWN"`; `total_amount` is a string or null.

#### BG-9 snapshot create (`api.py:512-539`, `snapshots.py:113-250`)
Body: `snapshot_type` (default `"BUSINESS"`; `BUSINESS|CUSTOMER|ORDER|PRODUCT|CAMPAIGN`, case-insensitive, else 400 `Unsupported snapshot_type 'X'; expected one of [...]`), `name` (optional; auto-generated e.g. `Business Graph Snapshot (YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM)`, `Customer Journey Snapshot #<id>`), `root_entity_id` (required int for non-BUSINESS, else 400 `root_entity_id is required for CUSTOMER snapshot`; non-integer -> 500), `root_entity_type` (ignored for non-BUSINESS, forced to the type; for BUSINESS it is stored verbatim), `filters` (object with the FILTERS keys; if `filters` is absent/empty the FILTERS keys are read from the top level of the body). **Synchronous**, one request; builds the graph in memory and writes one `graph_snapshots` row with the full serialized graph in `snapshot_data` (`{nodes[], edges[], timeline[], summary{}}`); BUSINESS type loads ALL nodes and edges of the business with no cap. `BUSINESS` snapshots IGNORE `filters` for content but still record them in `filters`. `valid_at`, `time_from`, `time_to` are applied (journey types) but NOT recorded in `snapshot.filters` (`snapshots.py:218-233`). Summary: BUSINESS `{"type":"FULL_BUSINESS_GRAPH","node_count","edge_count"}`; ORDER `{status,total_amount,node_count,edge_count}`; CUSTOMER/PRODUCT/CAMPAIGN = the journey summary. Response excludes `snapshot_data`. Snapshots are append-only (no delete/update endpoint); a second identical POST creates a second row.
`snapshot_data` shapes (`snapshots.py:66-110`): node `{id, entity_type, entity_id, canonical_identity_id, label, node_type, properties, first_seen_at, last_seen_at, created_at}`; edge `{id, source_node_id, target_node_id, relationship_type, confidence, properties:{rule_key, relationship_source, metadata}, valid_from, valid_to, business_relationship_id}`; timeline event `{entity_type, entity_id, label, stage, timestamp, properties, connecting_relationships:["-> TYPE","<- TYPE"]}`.
`GET snapshots/<id>/` returns the stored `filters` (null if none) and full `snapshot_data` unpaginated (potentially very large); stored `root_entity_type/root_entity_id` are NOT returned by either GET.

#### 2e. BG-11 rebuild: what it executes
Code: `GraphRebuildAPIView.post` (`api.py:577-598`) -> `rebuild_business_graph(business_id, clean)` (`sync.py:300-344`). **Synchronous**, one request, ONE `transaction.atomic()`; no queue/timeout; duration NOT VERIFIED (scales with row counts; per-row `filter(pk).first()` + `update_or_create`, `projection.py:188-238`). Steps, in order:
1. Business must exist else `GraphScopeError` (403).
2. If `clean` is true: delete ALL `GraphEdge` then ALL `GraphNode` of the business (counts become `edges_pruned`/`nodes_pruned`). Else `prune_stale_projections` (`sync.py:93-190`): delete expired edges (`valid_to <= now`), edges whose `BusinessRelationship` is gone or whose `rule_key` is outside the 23 supported keys, nodes whose canonical record no longer exists in this business, then dangling edges.
3. `project_business_graph` (`projection.py:348-420`): upsert a node for EVERY row of the 14 supported entity models of the business (all entity types, whether or not they have relationships), then for every `BusinessRelationship` with `rule_key IN SUPPORTED_RELATIONSHIP_RULES` project an edge (expired `valid_to <= now` -> existing edge deleted, counted in `edges_pruned`).
`clean` parsing: `bool(request.data.get("clean", False))` (`api.py:582`), so it MUST be sent as JSON `true`/`false`; the strings `"false"`, `"0"` or any form-encoded value are truthy. `clean:true` is destructive but snapshots survive (they hold their own JSON copy). Counter semantics: `nodes_updated`/`edges_updated` count every re-upserted existing row, changed or not. Any single invalid relationship (triplet mismatch with its rule, invalid `relationship_source`, missing endpoint entity, cross-business endpoint) raises and rolls back the WHOLE rebuild -> 400 `{"error": "<specific message>"}` (cross-tenant -> `GraphCrossBusinessError` 400, wrong-business -> `GraphScopeError` 403). Response is always 200 with the counts above.
**Hard dependency:** the graph reads existing `BusinessRelationship` rows; it does not create them. In the BI flow they come from BI-11 (`rebuild_relationships`). See GAP 1 about a rule-key mismatch that makes that hand-off produce zero edges.

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
### (3) ENUMS AND VOCABULARY (exact strings from code)

**Fact statuses** `FACT_STATUSES` (`facts_issues.py:63-73`): `"CANDIDATE"`, `"VERIFIED"`, `"INVALIDATED"`, `"EXPIRED"`. (API default on create: `VERIFIED`; only VERIFIED+in-window facts are readable over HTTP, see BI-3.)
**Fact types** documented (`facts_issues.py:75-82`, not enforced): `"REVENUE_CHANGE"`, `"DELIVERY_FAILURE_RATE"`, `"CAMPAIGN_PERFORMANCE"`, `"CONVERSION_DECLINE"`, `"SUPPORT_DELAY"`, `"CUSTOMER_LTV"`, `"ANOMALY_DETECTED"`. API default when omitted: `"GENERAL"` (api.py:303, not in the documented set).
**Issue severities** `ISSUE_SEVERITIES` (`facts_issues.py:84-94`): `"LOW"`, `"MEDIUM"`, `"HIGH"`, `"CRITICAL"` (API default `"MEDIUM"`).
**Issue statuses** `ISSUE_STATUSES` (`facts_issues.py:96-106`): `"OPEN"`, `"ACKNOWLEDGED"`, `"RESOLVED"`, `"DISMISSED"`.
**Issue types** documented `DOCUMENTED_ISSUE_TYPES` (`facts_issues.py:108-120`, not enforced): `"HIGH_DELIVERY_FAILURE"`, `"LOW_CAMPAIGN_ROAS"`, `"HIGH_CART_ABANDONMENT"`, `"SUPPORT_RESPONSE_DELAY"`, `"REVENUE_DECLINE"`.
**Metric categories** `CATEGORIES` (`metrics.py:174-176`): `"SALES"`, `"REVENUE"`, `"CUSTOMER"`, `"MARKETING"`, `"SUPPORT"`, `"WEBSITE"`, `"DELIVERY"`.
**Value types -> allowed units** `VALUE_TYPE_UNITS` (`metrics.py:180-189`):
- `COUNT`: `ORDERS, UNITS, CUSTOMERS, SESSIONS, CONVERSATIONS, SHIPMENTS, IMPRESSIONS, CLICKS, CONVERSIONS, EVENTS`
- `AMOUNT`: `CURRENCY`; `RATIO`: `RATIO`; `DURATION`: `SECONDS`; `NUMBER`: `ORDERS_PER_CUSTOMER`.
Conventions (`metrics.py:28-34`): ratios stored as fractions (0.25, not 25), durations in seconds, AMOUNT is one currency per row, values quantised to 6 decimals.
**Period types** `PERIOD_TYPES` (`metrics.py:194`): `"HOUR"`, `"DAY"`, `"WEEK"`, `"MONTH"`, `"CUSTOM"` (labels only; bounds are half-open `[period_start, period_end)`, not calendar-checked).
**Dimension types** `DIMENSION_TYPES` (`metrics.py:200-209`): ENTITY kind (value = canonical pk as digit string, no leading zeros): `PRODUCT`, `PRODUCT_VARIANT`, `CAMPAIGN`; LABEL kind (free text label): `REGION`, `COURIER`, `CHANNEL`, `CUSTOMER_SEGMENT`, `CURRENCY`. `dimension_type` and `dimension_value` are both set or both null (null = business-wide).
**Metric registry: 42 keys** (`metrics.py:303-756`; format `bi.metric.<name>.v1`; columns: key | display name | category | value_type | unit). These are exactly what BI-1 returns after sync:
```
bi.metric.sales_total_orders.v1 | Total Orders | SALES | COUNT | ORDERS
bi.metric.sales_units_sold.v1 | Units Sold | SALES | COUNT | UNITS
bi.metric.sales_average_order_value.v1 | Average Order Value | SALES | AMOUNT | CURRENCY
bi.metric.sales_cancellation_rate.v1 | Order Cancellation Rate | SALES | RATIO | RATIO
bi.metric.sales_growth_rate.v1 | Sales Growth Rate | SALES | RATIO | RATIO
bi.metric.revenue_gross.v1 | Gross Revenue | REVENUE | AMOUNT | CURRENCY
bi.metric.revenue_refunded.v1 | Refunded Revenue | REVENUE | AMOUNT | CURRENCY
bi.metric.revenue_net.v1 | Net Revenue | REVENUE | AMOUNT | CURRENCY
bi.metric.revenue_growth_rate.v1 | Revenue Growth Rate | REVENUE | RATIO | RATIO
bi.metric.revenue_per_customer.v1 | Revenue Per Customer | REVENUE | AMOUNT | CURRENCY
bi.metric.customer_new_customers.v1 | New Customers Acquired | CUSTOMER | COUNT | CUSTOMERS
bi.metric.customer_active_customers.v1 | Active Customers | CUSTOMER | COUNT | CUSTOMERS
bi.metric.customer_repeat_rate.v1 | Repeat Customer Rate | CUSTOMER | RATIO | RATIO

bi.metric.marketing_conversion_rate.v1 | Marketing Conversion Rate | MARKETING | RATIO | RATIO
bi.metric.marketing_attributed_revenue.v1 | Attributed Revenue | MARKETING | AMOUNT | CURRENCY
bi.metric.marketing_return_on_ad_spend.v1 | Return On Ad Spend (ROAS) | MARKETING | RATIO | RATIO
bi.metric.support_response_time.v1 | Support Response Time | SUPPORT | DURATION | SECONDS
bi.metric.support_resolution_time.v1 | Support Resolution Time | SUPPORT | DURATION | SECONDS
bi.metric.support_conversation_volume.v1 | Conversation Volume | SUPPORT | COUNT | CONVERSATIONS
bi.metric.support_unresolved_cases.v1 | Unresolved Support Cases | SUPPORT | COUNT | CONVERSATIONS

bi.metric.website_product_views.v1 | Product Views | WEBSITE | COUNT | EVENTS
bi.metric.website_conversion_rate.v1 | Website Conversion Rate | WEBSITE | RATIO | RATIO
```

Per-category counts: SALES 5, REVENUE 5, CUSTOMER 5, MARKETING 10, SUPPORT 5, WEBSITE 6, DELIVERY 6 (= 42). Descriptions exist in code (`metrics.py:303-756`, e.g. each states "stored as a fraction") but BI-1 does not return `description`.
**Calculation rule string** (service-only, not in API): `bi.metric.<name>.v1` or `bi.metric.<name>.v1.<lowercase_variant>` (`metrics.py:907-926`).

**Service-only vocabularies (never returned by any HTTP endpoint):**
- Snapshot write statuses `CREATED, UPDATED, UNCHANGED, NOT_RECORDED_UNAVAILABLE` (`metrics.py:217-223`); comparison directions `INCREASED, DECREASED, UNCHANGED, UNAVAILABLE` (`:225-231`).
- Anomaly: rule types `THRESHOLD, PERIOD_COMPARISON, BASELINE_DEVIATION, TREND_BREAK, CROSS_DOMAIN`; outcome statuses `ANOMALY_DETECTED, NORMAL, INSUFFICIENT_DATA, UNAVAILABLE`; expected directions `INCREASING, DECREASING, ANY`; reasons `MISSING_CURRENT_SNAPSHOT, MISSING_BASELINE_SNAPSHOT, INSUFFICIENT_BASELINE_VOLUME, INSUFFICIENT_BASELINE_PERIODS, INSUFFICIENT_TREND_OBSERVATIONS, NO_PRIOR_TREND_ESTABLISHED` (`anomalies.py:60-100`). Result object `AnomalyDetectionResult{is_anomaly,status,rule_id,rule_type,metric_key,severity,dimension_type,dimension_value,current_value,comparison_value,relative_change,absolute_change,evidence_snapshot_ids,message,details}` (`:160-177`).
- Attribution models `DIRECT, FIRST_TOUCH, LAST_TOUCH, PLATFORM_REPORTED`; provenance `DETERMINISTIC, PLATFORM_REPORTED, UNATTRIBUTED` (`attribution.py:43-62`).
- Website funnel stages `VIEW, CART, CHECKOUT, PURCHASE` (`website.py:44-49`); support statuses `OPEN, PENDING, RESOLVED, CLOSED`, priorities `LOW, NORMAL, HIGH, URGENT` (`support.py:69-75`); retention segments `NEW, REPEAT`; delivery segments `SUBMISSION_TO_DELIVERY, SUBMISSION_TO_PICKUP, PICKUP_TO_DELIVERY` (`delivery.py:338-340`); unavailable reasons such as `NO_DENOMINATOR, ZERO_DENOMINATOR, NO_DATA, MIXED_CURRENCY, MISSING_CONFIGURED_THRESHOLD`.
- BusinessRelationship: 23 rule keys prefixed `bi.relationship.<name>.v1` (BI writes) vs unprefixed `<name>` (graph reads), see GAP 1; `relationship_source` DETERMINISTIC; confidence fixed `1.0000`.

**Graph vocabulary:**
- Directions `VALID_DIRECTIONS`: `"OUTBOUND"`, `"INBOUND"`, `"BIDIRECTIONAL"` (`traversal.py:44`).
- Entity / node types `SUPPORTED_ENTITY_MODELS` (`projection.py:53-68`), 14: `CUSTOMER, PRODUCT, PRODUCT_VARIANT, ORDER, PAYMENT, SHIPMENT, CAMPAIGN, CAMPAIGN_CONVERSION, WEBSITE_VISITOR, WEBSITE_SESSION, WEBSITE_EVENT, CONVERSATION, MESSAGE, CALL`. `node_type` equals `entity_type`.
- Edge relationship types (18 distinct, from the 23 rules, `projection.py:72-96`): `PLACED_ORDER, CONTAINS_PRODUCT, CONTAINS_VARIANT, HAS_PAYMENT, HAS_SHIPMENT, HAS_CONVERSATION, HAS_MESSAGE, HAS_CALL, HAS_VISITOR, HAS_SESSION, HAS_EVENT, HAS_WEBSITE_EVENT, REFERENCES_PRODUCT, REFERENCES_ORDER, HAS_REPORTED_CONVERSION, REPORTED_CUSTOMER, REPORTED_ORDER, REPORTED_SESSION`. (Spec examples `PAID_FOR, ATTRIBUTED_TO, PARTICIPATED_IN, CONTACTED, SHIPPED_AS` do not exist anywhere in `app/business_graph` or `app/business_intelligence`.)
- Rule triples (source, relationship, target): `customer_order (CUSTOMER,PLACED_ORDER,ORDER)`, `order_product (ORDER,CONTAINS_PRODUCT,PRODUCT)`, `order_variant (ORDER,CONTAINS_VARIANT,PRODUCT_VARIANT)`, `order_payment (ORDER,HAS_PAYMENT,PAYMENT)`, `order_shipment (ORDER,HAS_SHIPMENT,SHIPMENT)`, `customer_conversation (CUSTOMER,HAS_CONVERSATION,CONVERSATION)`, `customer_message (CUSTOMER,HAS_MESSAGE,MESSAGE)`, `conversation_message (CONVERSATION,HAS_MESSAGE,MESSAGE)`, `customer_call (CUSTOMER,HAS_CALL,CALL)`, `conversation_call (CONVERSATION,HAS_CALL,CALL)`, `order_call (ORDER,HAS_CALL,CALL)`, `customer_visitor (CUSTOMER,HAS_VISITOR,WEBSITE_VISITOR)`, `visitor_session (WEBSITE_VISITOR,HAS_SESSION,WEBSITE_SESSION)`, `customer_session (CUSTOMER,HAS_SESSION,WEBSITE_SESSION)`, `session_event (WEBSITE_SESSION,HAS_EVENT,WEBSITE_EVENT)`, `customer_event (CUSTOMER,HAS_WEBSITE_EVENT,WEBSITE_EVENT)`, `event_product (WEBSITE_EVENT,REFERENCES_PRODUCT,PRODUCT)`, `event_order (WEBSITE_EVENT,REFERENCES_ORDER,ORDER)`, `session_order (WEBSITE_SESSION,REFERENCES_ORDER,ORDER)`, `campaign_conversion (CAMPAIGN,HAS_REPORTED_CONVERSION,CAMPAIGN_CONVERSION)`, `campaign_customer (CAMPAIGN,REPORTED_CUSTOMER,CUSTOMER)`, `campaign_order (CAMPAIGN,REPORTED_ORDER,ORDER)`, `campaign_session (CAMPAIGN,REPORTED_SESSION,WEBSITE_SESSION)`.
- `VALID_RELATIONSHIP_SOURCES` (`projection.py:98`): `DETERMINISTIC, REPORTED, DIRECT, PLATFORM_REPORTED`.
- Snapshot types `SUPPORTED_SNAPSHOT_TYPES` (`snapshots.py:49-55`): `BUSINESS, CUSTOMER, ORDER, PRODUCT, CAMPAIGN` (model default `BUSINESS`, `app/models.py:1269`).
- Journey timeline `stage` values `STAGE_MAPPING` (`journeys.py:41-56`): `MARKETING` (CAMPAIGN, CAMPAIGN_CONVERSION), `WEBSITE` (WEBSITE_VISITOR/SESSION/EVENT), `CUSTOMER`, `COMMERCE` (ORDER, PRODUCT, PRODUCT_VARIANT), `PAYMENT`, `FULFILLMENT` (SHIPMENT), `SUPPORT` (CONVERSATION, MESSAGE), `CALLING` (CALL); fallback `OTHER`.
- Journey kinds (URL segment): `customer`, `order`, `product`, `campaign`.
- Filter names: `entity_types, relationship_types, min_confidence, status, channel, provider, time_from, time_to, valid_at, dimensions`.
- Node `properties` keys: `status, channel, priority, currency, provider, event_type, direction, total_amount` (`projection.py:164`).

### (5) FRONTEND NOTES

1. **Nothing is callable today.** MOUNT PREFIX: NONE EXISTS. A FE plan should treat every BI/BG endpoint as BACKEND-BLOCKED until (a) both urls.py are `include()`d, and (b) something sets `request.business` (or `auth_business`) from authenticated membership. Until then every call is HTTP 403. The BI/BG routes do not contain a `business_id` path segment; the business is implicit from the request context (the `?business_id=` param is only a guard that must match).
2. **Auth model differs from the live domain views**: these views do not require login by themselves and do no role check. Once the seam exists, a logged-in session user needs a CSRF token on POST/PATCH (SessionAuthentication). Unauthenticated/forbidden = 403 JSON `{"detail": ...}`, never 401.
3. **Two error contracts.** BI: `{"error": "<generic sentence>"}` only; the frontend cannot tell validation causes apart (all validation = 400 `Invalid request parameters or unresolvable resource reference.`). BG: `{"error": "<specific sentence>"}` (show or log it; parse by status only). DRF-level problems use `{"detail": ...}`. HTTP 500 responses (uncaught exceptions) are non-JSON HTML in production; handle them separately. Cases that 500 today are listed in GAP 8.
4. **Pagination.** BI list endpoints (BI-1, BI-2, BI-3, BI-7): `limit` (default 50, max 200, min 1) + `offset`, envelope `{total_count, limit, offset, results}`. Server silently corrects bad numbers (does not 400). Sort orders are fixed (`key`, `-period_start`, `-detected_at`) without a tie-break, so equal timestamps may reorder between pages. BG: BG-8 returns an unpaginated bare array; BG-1/2/3 are capped only by caller-supplied `max_nodes`/`limit`/`max_depth` (no server cap); BG-10 returns the entire stored graph.
5. **Filtering.** BI-3: `status`, `fact_type`; BI-7: `status`, `severity`, `issue_type`; BI-2: `period_type`, `dimension_type`. Unknown filter values return empty results, not errors. Status/severity accepted in any case for filters (upper-cased) and bodies. BG filters: see FILTERS table; send comma-separated lists for `entity_types`/`relationship_types`; send timezone-qualified datetimes (`2026-01-01T00:00:00Z`); `dimensions` only via POST JSON.
6. **Write-on-read and side effects.**
   - `GET metrics/` (BI-1) creates/repairs 42 rows per business (not read-only; first call is slower). BI-2 returns 404 for any registry key until BI-1 or BI-11 has run once per business.
   - `POST pipeline/run/` (BI-11) rewrites `business_relationships` (synchronous, unbounded, row-locks the Business on PostgreSQL), registers metrics, and returns counts only. It does NOT produce metric snapshots, facts or issues. A 200 with `quarantined_records: 1` and all-zero counts means the entire reconcile was rolled back by an invalid reference. Long-running: disable/reflect a loading state; no job id/poll endpoint exists.
   - `POST rebuild/` (BG-11) is synchronous in one transaction; `clean:true` wipes the business's graph first. Send `clean` as JSON boolean only. Journeys/traversal return 400 "has not been projected" until BG-11 has run.
   - `POST snapshots/` (BG-9) is synchronous and can store a very large JSON blob; BG-8 never shows `snapshot_data`, BG-10 returns all of it.
   - POST facts/issues are UPSERTS: reuse of (`fact_type`,`rule_key`) or (`issue_type`,`rule_key`) updates the same row. Use 201 vs 200 and `created` to distinguish. Always send a distinct `rule_key` for independent facts/issues.
7. **Fact visibility quirk (UI must plan for it).** Over HTTP only VERIFIED, in-window facts are ever listed/readable. Setting a fact to `CANDIDATE|INVALIDATED|EXPIRED` (BI-6) makes it disappear from BI-3/BI-5 and it cannot be edited again. A "history of invalidated facts" or "restore fact" UI is not buildable on this API. Issues have no such restriction (all four statuses listable; reopen allowed).
8. **Field formats to render.** Decimals are strings (do not `parseFloat` blindly for `value`; ratios are fractions 0..1, durations seconds, amounts a single currency whose code is NOT returned by BI-2). `period_start/period_end/detected_at/...` ISO-8601 with `+00:00`. `evidence`/`metadata` are free-form JSON objects (may contain `status_change_reason`, `resolution_notes`, `fact_ids`, `snapshot_ids`). Snapshot `dimension_value` for PRODUCT/PRODUCT_VARIANT/CAMPAIGN is an id string, so names require a separate lookup. BI-2 does not return the metric's unit; join with BI-1 by `metric_key`.
9. **Graph rendering.** Node ids in `edges[].source_node_id/target_node_id` are GraphNode pks (= `nodes[].id`), not entity ids. When `truncated` is true, drop edges whose endpoint is missing from `nodes`. Treat `confidence` as a decimal string. Order journey `customer_node_id` is a GraphNode pk. Entity type strings are upper-case and fixed (section 3); labels are server-built.
10. **Evidence UX is thin.** Path `explain=true` is the only evidence-bearing graph call; fact/issue context there will be empty for records created through BI-4/BI-8.
11. **Backend-blocked capabilities for the FE plan** (no HTTP surface at all): relationship browsing, any metric calculation/refresh (revenue, sales, customer, marketing/campaign, support, website/conversion, delivery), attribution views, product/customer/retention analyses, anomaly detection/rules, automatic fact/issue generation, fact/issue evidence rows, metric registry definitions (descriptions), snapshot compare/trend, metric snapshot writes, graph node/edge direct lookup, incremental graph sync, snapshot delete/compare, graph metric/attribution/identity evidence.
12. **Adjacent LIVE surfaces touching the same tables** (outside this scan's scope; only opened, not audited): `GET businesses/<int:business_id>/issues/` (`app/urls.py:33-37`, `app/views.py:146-152`): lists the business's `status="OPEN"` `BusinessIssue` rows via `BusinessIssueSerializer` (`app/serializers/action.py:63-82`: id, business, issue_type, subject_entity_type, subject_entity_id, title, description, severity, confidence, status, rule_key, detected_at, resolved_at, created_at, updated_at), `IsAuthenticated` + `require_business_access`. `GET businesses/<int:business_id>/graph-context/<slug:kind>/<int:pk>/` (`app/urls.py:113`, `app/domain_views.py:311-313`, `app/domains/projections.py:183-197`): read-only persisted one-hop graph context. These do not need the unmounted modules.

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
### (6) GAPS AND AMBIGUITIES

1. **BI -> Graph hand-off is broken by a rule-key mismatch (verified by reading both sides).** BI writes `BusinessRelationship.rule_key = "bi.relationship.<name>.v1"` (`relationships.py:70`, assigned at `:339`). The graph projects/prunes only rows whose `rule_key` is in `SUPPORTED_RELATIONSHIP_RULES.keys()` = unprefixed `"customer_order"`, `"order_product"`, ... (`projection.py:72-96, 392`; `sync.py:131, 245`). The only places that create unprefixed keys are the graph tests (`app/business_graph/tests/*.py`), `bi.relationship` appears nowhere else, and the other non-test writer (`app/overview/demo.py:88-90`) uses `rule_key='demo.attribution.v1'` with lower-case entity types. So after BI-11, BG-11 would create nodes but ZERO edges from production-derived relationships (inferred, not executed). Journeys/paths would show isolated nodes.
2. **Tenant seam does not exist** (ground truth #4): every endpoint returns 403 even when mounted. Open design questions: where `request.business` comes from, and who checks membership/roles (these views do neither; they also bypass `IsAuthenticated`).
3. **Mount prefix undecided.** Route names are `intelligence-*` and `graph-*`. The two URLconfs have no overlapping paths with each other. No existing URL in `app/urls.py` collides with the relative paths (the existing issues route is `businesses/<int:business_id>/issues/`).
4. **Pipeline run is not the pipeline.** Docstring (api.py:19) and the spec flow promise relationships -> metrics -> anomalies -> facts -> issues; code does relationships + registry sync + two counts only. No metric snapshots are ever produced through HTTP, and no non-test code outside the BI package calls any calculator (grep: callers of `record_metric_snapshot` exist only inside `app/business_intelligence/{campaign,customer_product,support,revenue,delivery,website}.py`, none invoked from a view, command, signal or task). So BI-2/BI-3/BI-7 will be empty unless data is injected by scripts or by the manual POSTs.
5. **Fact manager hides non-VERIFIED facts** from every BI fact read/PATCH (section 2b notes). Whether this is intended is ambiguous; it contradicts the API accepting `status=CANDIDATE|INVALIDATED|EXPIRED` filters and the 4-state lifecycle. The upsert lookup in `create_or_update_fact` also uses the hidden-filter manager, so an INVALIDATED fact is not found and a new duplicate row is created on the next POST.
6. **Quarantine claim vs code.** api.py:13-15 claims per-row quarantine; implementation rolls back the whole rebuild and reports `quarantined_records: 1`, hiding the reason (logged only). `RelationshipConflictError` is not quarantined -> 500.
7. **SEC-MED-1 anti-oracle only partial**: PATCH unknown id = 400, PATCH other-tenant id = 403, GET both = 404 (distinguishable). Graph API explicitly leaks the owning business id in 403 text (BG errors), contradicting the BI approach and the BG docstring's "no trust in caller business" intent. `fact_ids`/`snapshot_ids` on BI-8 accept foreign-tenant ids without checks.
8. **Uncaught 500 sources (inferred, not run):** BI: non-numeric `value`/`confidence` (`decimal.InvalidOperation`), non-string `statement`/`severity`/`status`, non-object `evidence`, array JSON body (`request.data.get` AttributeError), missing `issue_type` (NOT NULL), over-length strings on PostgreSQL (`title` 255, `rule_key` 100, `value_unit` 30, `fact_type`/`issue_type` 100), `MetricSnapshotConflictError`, `RelationshipConflictError`. BG: non-integer `max_depth`, `max_nodes`, `entity_id`, `limit`, `root_entity_id` (bare `int()`), invalid-but-well-formed datetimes (`parse_datetime` ValueError), naive datetimes (naive/aware comparison TypeError), negative `limit` on `query/` mode B.
9. **No server caps on graph size**: `max_depth`, `max_nodes`, `limit` unbounded; docstring claims "strictly capped". BFS issues a query per expanded node; `snapshots/` BUSINESS type and `rebuild/` are unbounded synchronous work in a `runserver` process.
10. **`status="COMPLETED"` is hard-coded**; BI-11 also returns 200 for a fully rolled-back run. FE cannot distinguish "nothing to do" from "rolled back" except via `quarantined_records > 0`.
11. **Graph snapshot metadata inconsistencies:** BUSINESS snapshots ignore `filters` for content but store them; `valid_at/time_from/time_to` not recorded; stored `root_entity_type/id` not returned by any GET; BUSINESS snapshots store caller-supplied `root_entity_*` verbatim. `snapshots/` POST code has an unreachable duplicate `return` (api.py:541-551), harmless.
12. **Spec-vs-code vocabulary drift:** spec edge types (`PAID_FOR`, `ATTRIBUTED_TO`, `PARTICIPATED_IN`, `CONTACTED`, `SHIPPED_AS`) are not implemented; code types are the 18 listed in section 3. Spec "Business Dimension Filtering" (campaign/product/region/courier/segment) is only a generic equality match on 8 node-property keys.
13. **Legacy metric rows** (e.g. `DAILY_PERFORMANCE`, `app/services/bi.py:66-70`) share `business_metrics` and will be listed by BI-1 (no filter on `is_active`/prefix); their `key` does not match `bi.metric.<name>.v<n>`, and BI-2 would accept them in the path (route is `<str:metric_key>`).
14. **Unused imports in BI api.py** (`AnomalyRuleConfig`, `evaluate_anomaly_rule`, `FACT_STATUSES`, `FACT_STATUS_VERIFIED`, `ISSUE_SEVERITIES`, `ISSUE_STATUSES`, `get_metric_definition`, `list_metric_definitions`, `record_metric_snapshot`, `RelationshipConflictError`): signal that anomaly/registry/snapshot-write endpoints were planned but not built.
15. **Not verified (would need execution):** real response times, the exact `value` string format on PostgreSQL vs SQLite (test expects `"1500.000000"`; SQLite float storage can lose precision beyond about 15 significant digits, `metrics.py:70-74`), the actual 500 behaviours listed in GAP 8, and any behaviour once the settings/import errors (ground truth #3) are fixed.
